class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UN_WPP_2022-194">&#91;194&#93;</a></sup></td></tr></tbody></table><p>As of 2019, Ghana has a population of 30,083,000.<sup id="cite_ref-195" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-195">&#91;195&#93;</a></sup> Around 29% of the population is under the age of 15, while persons aged 15–64 make up 57.8 percent of the population.<sup id="cite_ref-databank.worldbank.org_196-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-databank.worldbank.org-196">&#91;196&#93;</a></sup> The 2010 census reported that the largest ethnic groups are the Akan (47.3%), the Mole-Dagbani (16.6%), the Ewe (13.9%), the Ga-Dangme (7.4%), the Gurma (5.7%) and the Guan (3.7%).<sup id="cite_ref-statsghana.gov.gh_197-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-statsghana.gov.gh-197">&#91;197&#93;</a></sup> </p><div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Bev%C3%B6lkerungspyramide_Ghana_2016.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f4/Bev%C3%B6lkerungspyramide_Ghana_2016.png/220px-Bev%C3%B6lkerungspyramide_Ghana_2016.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="153" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f4/Bev%C3%B6lkerungspyramide_Ghana_2016.png/330px-Bev%C3%B6lkerungspyramide_Ghana_2016.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f4/Bev%C3%B6lkerungspyramide_Ghana_2016.png/440px-Bev%C3%B6lkerungspyramide_Ghana_2016.png 2x" data-file-width="610" data-file-height="425" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Bev%C3%B6lkerungspyramide_Ghana_2016.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Population pyramid 2016</div></div></div>
<p>The median age of Ghanaian citizens is 30 years old and the average household size is 3.6 persons.
</p><p>
With <a href="/wiki/Immigration_to_Ghana" title="Immigration to Ghana">recent legal immigration</a> of <a href="/wiki/Skilled_worker" title="Skilled worker">skilled workers</a> who possess <a href="/wiki/Ghana_Card" title="Ghana Card">Ghana Cards</a>, there is a small population of Chinese, Malaysian, Indian, Middle Eastern and European nationals. In 2010, the <a href="/wiki/Ghana_Immigration_Service" title="Ghana Immigration Service">Ghana Immigration Service</a> reported many <a href="/wiki/Economic_migrant" title="Economic migrant">economic migrants</a> and <a href="/wiki/Illegal_immigration_to_Ghana" title="Illegal immigration to Ghana">Illegal immigrants inhabiting Ghana</a>: 14.6% (or 3.1&#160;million) of Ghana's 2010 population (predominantly Nigerians, Burkinabe citizens, Togolese citizens, and Malian citizens). In 1969, under the "Ghana Aliens Compliance Order" enacted by the Prime Minister Kofi Abrefa Busia;<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_Owes_no_Apology_to_Anybody_for_Aliens_Compliance_Order_198-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_Owes_no_Apology_to_Anybody_for_Aliens_Compliance_Order-198">&#91;198&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Border_Guard_Unit" title="Border Guard Unit">Border Guard Unit</a> deported over 3,000,000 aliens and illegal immigrants in three months as they made up 20% of the population at the time.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_Owes_no_Apology_to_Anybody_for_Aliens_Compliance_Order_198-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_Owes_no_Apology_to_Anybody_for_Aliens_Compliance_Order-198">&#91;198&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-The_History_of_Ghana&#39;s_1969_Aliens_Compliance_Order_199-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_History_of_Ghana&#39;s_1969_Aliens_Compliance_Order-199">&#91;199&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-200" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-200">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup>In 2013, there was a mass deportation of illegal miners, more than 4,000 of them Chinese nationals.<sup id="cite_ref-201" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-201">&#91;201&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-202" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-202">&#91;202&#93;</a></sup></p><div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Ghana_Card_biometric.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/9e/Ghana_Card_biometric.jpg/220px-Ghana_Card_biometric.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="152" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/9e/Ghana_Card_biometric.jpg/330px-Ghana_Card_biometric.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/9e/Ghana_Card_biometric.jpg/440px-Ghana_Card_biometric.jpg 2x" data-file-width="928" data-file-height="640" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Ghana_Card_biometric.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Ghana_Card" title="Ghana Card">Ghana Card</a> (Ghanaian electronic ID Card)–obverse with <a href="/wiki/Biometric_passport" title="Biometric passport">chip</a></div></div></div>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Languages">Languages</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ghana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Languages">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Languages_of_Ghana" title="Languages of Ghana">Languages of Ghana</a></div>
<p>English is the <a href="/wiki/Official_language" title="Official language">official language</a> of Ghana.<sup id="cite_ref-USA_203-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-USA-203">&#91;203&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-204" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-204">&#91;204&#93;</a></sup> Additionally, there are eleven languages that have the status of government-sponsored languages:
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Akan_language" title="Akan language">Akan languages</a> (<a href="/wiki/Asante_dialect" title="Asante dialect">Asante Twi</a>, <a href="/wiki/Akuapem_Twi" class="mw-redirect" title="Akuapem Twi">Akuapem Twi</a>, <a href="/wiki/Fante_dialect" title="Fante dialect">Fante</a> which have a high degree of mutual intelligibility, and <a href="/wiki/Nzema_language" title="Nzema language">Nzema</a>, which is less intelligible with the above)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Adangme_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Adangme language">Dangme</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Ewe_language" title="Ewe language">Ewe</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Ga_language" title="Ga language">Ga</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Gua_language" title="Gua language">Guan</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Kasem_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Kasem language">Kasem</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Oti-Volta_languages" class="mw-redirect" title="Oti-Volta languages">Mole-Dagbani</a> languages (<a href="/wiki/Dagaare" class="mw-redirect" title="Dagaare">Dagaare</a> and <a href="/wiki/Dagomba_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Dagomba language">Dagbanli</a>)<sup id="cite_ref-NCA_205-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NCA-205">&#91;205&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-206" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-206">&#91;206&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<p>Of these, Asante Twi is the most widely spoken.<sup id="cite_ref-Introduction_To_The_Verbal_and_Multi-Verbalsystem_of_Akan_207-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Introduction_To_The_Verbal_and_Multi-Verbalsystem_of_Akan-207">&#91;207&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Because Ghana is surrounded by <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_and_territories_where_French_is_an_official_language" title="List of countries and territories where French is an official language">French-speaking countries</a>, French is widely taught in schools and used for commercial and international economic exchanges. Since 2006, Ghana has been an associate member of the <a href="/wiki/Organisation_internationale_de_la_Francophonie" title="Organisation internationale de la Francophonie">Organisation internationale de la Francophonie</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-208" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-208">&#91;208&#93;</a></sup> the global organisation that unites French-speaking countries (84 nations on six continents). In 2005, more than 350,000 Ghanaian children studied French in schools. Since then, its status has been progressively updated to a mandatory language in every junior high school,<sup id="cite_ref-209" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-209">&#91;209&#93;</a></sup> and it is in the process of becoming an official language.<sup id="cite_ref-210" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-210">&#91;210&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-211" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-211">&#91;211&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Ghanaian_Pidgin_English" title="Ghanaian Pidgin English">Ghanaian Pidgin English</a>, also known as Kru English (or in Akan, <i>kroo brofo</i>), is a variety of <a href="/wiki/West_African_Pidgin_English" title="West African Pidgin English">West African Pidgin English</a> spoken mainly in Accra and in the southern towns.<sup id="cite_ref-Huber_1999_p139_212-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Huber_1999_p139-212">&#91;212&#93;</a></sup> It can be divided into two varieties, referred to as "uneducated" or "non-institutionalized" pidgin and "educated" or "institutionalized" pidgin, the former associated with uneducated or illiterate people and the latter acquired and used in institutions such as universities.<sup id="cite_ref-Huber_1999_p138-153_213-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Huber_1999_p138-153-213">&#91;213&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ghana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_Ghana" title="Religion in Ghana">Religion in Ghana</a></div>

<div style="clear:both;"></div><p>In 2010, the population was 72.2% Christian (24.3% <a href="/wiki/Pentecostalism" title="Pentecostalism">Pentecostal</a>, 18.4% <a href="/wiki/Protestantism" title="Protestantism">Protestant</a>, 13.1% <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic</a> and 11.4% other). Approximately 18.6% of the population of Ghana are Muslim,<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana:_CIA_World_FactBook_20-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana:_CIA_World_FactBook-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> (51% <a href="/wiki/Sunni_Islam" title="Sunni Islam">Sunni</a>, 16% <a href="/wiki/Ahmadiyya" title="Ahmadiyya">Ahmadiyya</a>, and 8% <a href="/wiki/Shia_Islam" title="Shia Islam">Shia</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-religion2010_220-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-religion2010-220">&#91;217&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-pew_221-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pew-221">&#91;218&#93;</a></sup> Hinduism in Ghana was popularized by Swami Ghana Nanda ji, who opened several temples in the nation.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> The temple of Lord Shiva in Accra is one of the largest where there are celebrations to Ganesh Chaturthi, Rath Yatra, and other Hindu observations.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> The <a href="/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_Faith" title="Baháʼí Faith">Bahá’í</a> religious community, established in Ghana in 1951, today includes more than 100 communities and over 50 local Bahá’í administrative councils, called Local Spiritual Assemblies.<sup id="cite_ref-222" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-222">&#91;219&#93;</a></sup>
</p><h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Universal_health_care_and_life_expectancy">Universal health care and life expectancy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ghana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Universal health care and life expectancy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Health_in_Ghana" title="Health in Ghana">Health in Ghana</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Eye_care_in_Ghana" title="Eye care in Ghana">Eye care in Ghana</a> and <a href="/wiki/Optometry_in_Ghana" title="Optometry in Ghana">Optometry in Ghana</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Life_expectancy_in_Ghana.svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Life_expectancy_in_Ghana.svg/220px-Life_expectancy_in_Ghana.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="155" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Life_expectancy_in_Ghana.svg/330px-Life_expectancy_in_Ghana.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Life_expectancy_in_Ghana.svg/440px-Life_expectancy_in_Ghana.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="850" data-file-height="600" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Life_expectancy_in_Ghana.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Development of life expectancy, 1921 to 2019</div></div></div>
<p>Ghana has a <a href="/wiki/Universal_health_care" title="Universal health care">universal health care</a> system strictly designated for <a href="/wiki/Ghanaian_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Ghanaian people">Ghanaian nationals</a>, <a href="/wiki/National_Health_Insurance_Scheme_(Ghana)" title="National Health Insurance Scheme (Ghana)">National Health Insurance Scheme</a> (NHIS).<sup id="cite_ref-223" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-223">&#91;220&#93;</a></sup> Health care is variable throughout Ghana and in 2012, over 12&#160;million Ghanaian nationals were covered by the NHIS.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana:_National_Health_Insurance_Scheme_(NHIS)_224-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana:_National_Health_Insurance_Scheme_(NHIS)-224">&#91;221&#93;</a></sup> Urban centres are well served and contain most of the hospitals, clinics, and pharmacies. There are over 200 hospitals, and Ghana is a destination for <a href="/wiki/Medical_tourism" title="Medical tourism">medical tourism</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-225" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-225">&#91;222&#93;</a></sup> In 2010, there were 0.1 physicians per 1,000 people and as of 2011<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Ghana&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, 0.9 hospital beds per 1,000 people.<sup id="cite_ref-databank.worldbank.org_196-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-databank.worldbank.org-196">&#91;196&#93;</a></sup> 5.2% of Ghana's GDP was spent on health in 2010.<sup id="cite_ref-Field_Listing:_Health_expenditures_226-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Field_Listing:_Health_expenditures-226">&#91;223&#93;</a></sup> In 2020, the WHO announced Ghana became the second country in the WHO African Region to attain regulatory system "maturity level 3", the second-highest in the four-tiered WHO classification of <a href="/w/index.php?title=National_medicines_regulatory_systems&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="National medicines regulatory systems (page does not exist)">National medicines regulatory systems</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-227" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-227">&#91;224&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Life expectancy at birth in 2020 was 71 for a female and 65 for a male.<sup id="cite_ref-CDC_228-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CDC-228">&#91;225&#93;</a></sup> In 2013, <a href="/wiki/Infant_mortality" title="Infant mortality">infant mortality</a> was to 39 per 1,000 live births.<sup id="cite_ref-Listing:_Infant_mortality_rate_229-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Listing:_Infant_mortality_rate-229">&#91;226&#93;</a></sup> Sources vary on life expectancy at birth; the <a href="/wiki/World_Health_Organization" title="World Health Organization">World Health Organization</a> (WHO) estimated 62 years for men and 64 years for women born in 2016.<sup id="cite_ref-230" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-230">&#91;227&#93;</a></sup> The fertility rate declined from 3.99 (2000) to 3.28 (2010) with 2.78 in urban region and 3.94 in rural region.<sup id="cite_ref-statsghana.gov.gh_197-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-statsghana.gov.gh-197">&#91;197&#93;</a></sup> The United Nations reports a fertility decline from 6.95 (1970) to 4.82 (2000) to 3.93 live births per woman in 2017.<sup id="cite_ref-231" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-231">&#91;228&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As of 2012<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Ghana&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, the <a href="/wiki/HIV/AIDS" title="HIV/AIDS">HIV/AIDS</a> prevalence was estimated at 1.40% among adults aged 15–49.<sup id="cite_ref-232" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-232">&#91;229&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ghana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:FB_20151104_19_47_22_Saved_Picture(1).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/69/FB_20151104_19_47_22_Saved_Picture%281%29.jpg/220px-FB_20151104_19_47_22_Saved_Picture%281%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/69/FB_20151104_19_47_22_Saved_Picture%281%29.jpg/330px-FB_20151104_19_47_22_Saved_Picture%281%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/69/FB_20151104_19_47_22_Saved_Picture%281%29.jpg/440px-FB_20151104_19_47_22_Saved_Picture%281%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="720" data-file-height="540" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:FB_20151104_19_47_22_Saved_Picture(1).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Hogbetsotso_festival" title="Hogbetsotso festival">Hogbetsotso festival</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Volta_region" class="mw-redirect" title="Volta region">Volta region</a></div></div></div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Ghana" title="Culture of Ghana">Culture of Ghana</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Food_and_drink">Food and drink</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ghana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Food and drink">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p><a href="/wiki/Ghanaian_cuisine" title="Ghanaian cuisine">Ghanaian cuisine</a> includes an assortment of soups and stews with varied seafoods; most Ghanaian soups are prepared with vegetables, meat, poultry or fish.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_cuisine,_banku,_okra_and_soup_233-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_cuisine,_banku,_okra_and_soup-233">&#91;230&#93;</a></sup> Fish is important in the diet with tilapia, roasted and fried <a href="/wiki/Whitebait" title="Whitebait">whitebait</a>, smoked fish and crayfish, all being common components of Ghanaian dishes.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_cuisine,_banku,_okra_and_soup_233-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_cuisine,_banku,_okra_and_soup-233">&#91;230&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Banku_(dish)" title="Banku (dish)">Banku</a> (akple) is a common starchy food made from ground corn (maize),<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_cuisine,_banku,_okra_and_soup_233-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_cuisine,_banku,_okra_and_soup-233">&#91;230&#93;</a></sup> and cornmeal based staples kɔmi (<a href="/wiki/Kenkey" title="Kenkey">kenkey</a>) and banku (akple) are usually accompanied by some form of fried fish (chinam) or grilled tilapia and a very spicy condiment made from raw red and green chillies, onions and tomatoes (pepper sauce).<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_cuisine,_banku,_okra_and_soup_233-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_cuisine,_banku,_okra_and_soup-233">&#91;230&#93;</a></sup> Banku and tilapia is a combo served in most restaurants.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_cuisine,_banku,_okra_and_soup_233-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_cuisine,_banku,_okra_and_soup-233">&#91;230&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Fufu" title="Fufu">Fufu</a> is the most common exported Ghanaian dish and is a delicacy across the <a href="/wiki/African_diaspora" title="African diaspora">African diaspora</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_cuisine,_banku,_okra_and_soup_233-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_cuisine,_banku,_okra_and_soup-233">&#91;230&#93;</a></sup> Rice is an established staple meal across the country, with various rice based dishes serving as breakfast, lunch and dinner, the main variants are waakye, plain rice and stew (eight kontomire or tomato gravy), fried rice and <a href="/wiki/Jollof_rice" title="Jollof rice">jollof rice</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-234" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-234">&#91;231&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Literature">Literature</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ghana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Literature">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="excerpt-block"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1066933788">.mw-parser-output .excerpt-hat .mw-editsection-like{font-style:normal}</style><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable dablink excerpt-hat selfref">This section is an excerpt from <a href="/wiki/Ghanaian_literature" title="Ghanaian literature">Ghanaian literature</a>.<span class="mw-editsection-like plainlinks"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Ghanaian_literature&amp;action=edit">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div><div class="excerpt">
<p><a href="/wiki/Ghanaian_literature" title="Ghanaian literature">Ghanaian literature</a> is literature produced by authors from Ghana or in the <a href="/wiki/Ghanaians" title="Ghanaians">Ghanaian diaspora</a>. The tradition of literature starts with a long oral tradition, was influence heavily by western literature during colonial rule, and became prominent with a post-colonial nationalist tradition in the mid 20th century.<sup id="cite_ref-235" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-235">&#91;232&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_literature_:0_236-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_literature_:0-236">&#91;233&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_literature_:1_237-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_literature_:1-237">&#91;234&#93;</a></sup> The current literary community continues with a diverse network of voices both within and outside the country today, including film, theatre, and modern digital formats such as blogging.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_literature_:0_236-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_literature_:0-236">&#91;233&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_literature_:1_237-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_literature_:1-237">&#91;234&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The most prominent authors are novelists <a href="/wiki/J._E._Casely_Hayford" title="J. E. Casely Hayford">J. E. Casely Hayford</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ayi_Kwei_Armah" title="Ayi Kwei Armah">Ayi Kwei Armah</a> and <a href="/wiki/Nii_Ayikwei_Parkes" class="mw-redirect" title="Nii Ayikwei Parkes">Nii Ayikwei Parkes</a>, who gained international acclaim with the books <i><a href="/wiki/Ethiopia_Unbound" title="Ethiopia Unbound">Ethiopia Unbound</a></i> (1911), <i><a href="/wiki/The_Beautyful_Ones_Are_Not_Yet_Born" title="The Beautyful Ones Are Not Yet Born">The Beautyful Ones Are Not Yet Born</a></i> (1968) and <i>Tail of the Blue Bird</i> (2009), respectively.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_literature_LIT_238-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_literature_LIT-238">&#91;235&#93;</a></sup> In addition to novels, other literature arts such as theatre and poetry have also had a very good development and support at the national level with prominent playwrights and poets <a href="/wiki/Joe_de_Graft" title="Joe de Graft">Joe de Graft</a> and <a href="/wiki/Efua_Sutherland" title="Efua Sutherland">Efua Sutherland</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_literature_LIT_238-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_literature_LIT-238">&#91;235&#93;</a></sup> 
</p>
The Ghanaian national literature radio programme and accompanying publication <i><a href="/wiki/Voices_of_Ghana" title="Voices of Ghana">Voices of Ghana</a> (1955-1957)</i> was one of the earliest on the African continent, and helped establish the  scope of the contemporary literary tradition in Ghana.<sup id="cite_ref-239" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-239">&#91;236&#93;</a></sup> Scholarship of Anglo-phone Africa sometimes favors literatures from other geographies, such as the <a href="/wiki/Nigerian_literature" title="Nigerian literature">literature of Nigeria</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-240" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-240">&#91;237&#93;</a></sup></div></div> 
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Clothing">Clothing</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ghana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: Clothing">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Adinkra_motifs_Rattray_1927.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2a/Adinkra_motifs_Rattray_1927.jpg/220px-Adinkra_motifs_Rattray_1927.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="121" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2a/Adinkra_motifs_Rattray_1927.jpg/330px-Adinkra_motifs_Rattray_1927.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/2/2a/Adinkra_motifs_Rattray_1927.jpg 2x" data-file-width="390" data-file-height="214" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Adinkra_motifs_Rattray_1927.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Adinkra_symbols" title="Adinkra symbols">Adinkra symbols</a> by <a href="/wiki/Robert_Sutherland_Rattray" title="Robert Sutherland Rattray">Robert Sutherland Rattray</a></div></div></div><p>
During the 13th century, Ghanaians developed their unique art of <i><a href="/wiki/Adinkra_symbols" title="Adinkra symbols">adinkra</a></i> printing. Hand-printed and hand-embroidered adinkra clothes were made and used exclusively by royalty for devotional ceremonies. Each of the motifs that make up the <a href="/wiki/Text_corpus" title="Text corpus">corpus</a> of adinkra symbolism has a name and meaning derived from a proverb, a historical event, human attitude, <a href="/wiki/Ethology" title="Ethology">ethology</a>, <a href="/wiki/Plant_life-form" title="Plant life-form">plant life-form</a>, or shapes of inanimate and man-made objects. The meanings of the motifs may be categorised into aesthetics, ethics, human relations, and concepts.<sup id="cite_ref-LIT_241-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-LIT-241">&#91;238&#93;</a></sup> The Adinkra symbols have a decorative function as tattoos but also represent objects that encapsulate evocative messages that convey traditional wisdom, aspects of life, or the environment. There are many symbols with distinct meanings, often linked with proverbs. In the words of <a href="/wiki/Kwame_Anthony_Appiah" title="Kwame Anthony Appiah">Anthony Appiah</a>, they were one of the means in a pre-literate society for "supporting the transmission of a complex and nuanced body of practice and belief".<sup id="cite_ref-242" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-242">&#91;239&#93;</a></sup></p><div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Kent_wove.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5b/Kent_wove.jpg/170px-Kent_wove.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="235" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5b/Kent_wove.jpg/255px-Kent_wove.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5b/Kent_wove.jpg/340px-Kent_wove.jpg 2x" data-file-width="371" data-file-height="513" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Kent_wove.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Kente cloth, the traditional or national cloth of Ghana, is worn by most southern Ghanaian ethnic groups, including the <a href="/wiki/Akan_people" title="Akan people">Akan</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Ga-Adangbe_people" title="Ga-Adangbe people">Ga</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Ewe_people" title="Ewe people">Ewe</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>Along with the <i>adinkra cloth,</i> Ghanaians use many cloth fabrics for their traditional attire.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth_243-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth-243">&#91;240&#93;</a></sup> The different ethnic groups have their own individual cloth. The most well known is the <a href="/wiki/Kente_cloth" title="Kente cloth">Kente cloth</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth_243-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth-243">&#91;240&#93;</a></sup> Kente is a very important national costume and clothing, and these clothes are used to make traditional and modern Kente attire.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth_243-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth-243">&#91;240&#93;</a></sup> Different symbols and different colours mean different things.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth_243-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth-243">&#91;240&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Kente_cloth" title="Kente cloth">Kente</a> is the most famous of all the Ghanaian clothes.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth_243-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth-243">&#91;240&#93;</a></sup> Kente is a ceremonial cloth hand-woven on a horizontal <a href="/wiki/Loom" title="Loom">treadle loom</a> and strips measuring about 4&#160;inches wide are sewn together into larger pieces of cloths.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth_243-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth-243">&#91;240&#93;</a></sup> Cloths come in various colours, sizes and designs and are worn during very important social and religious occasions.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth_243-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth-243">&#91;240&#93;</a></sup> In a cultural context, kente is more important than just a cloth as it is a visual representation of history and also a form of written language through weaving.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth_243-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth-243">&#91;240&#93;</a></sup> The term kente has its roots in the Akan word <i>kɛntɛn</i> which means a basket and the first kente weavers used raffia fibres to weave cloths that looked like kenten (a basket); and thus were referred to as <i>kenten ntoma</i>; meaning basket cloth.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth_243-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth-243">&#91;240&#93;</a></sup> The original Akan name of the cloth was <i>nsaduaso</i> or <i>nwontoma</i>, meaning "a cloth hand-woven on a loom"; however, "kente" is the most frequently used term today.
Kente is also woven by the Ewe people (Ewe Kente) in the Volta Region. The main weaving centers are Agortime area and Agbozume. Agbozume has a vibrant kente market attracting patrons from all over west Africa and the diaspora.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth_243-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth-243">&#91;240&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti"/><div class="thumb tmulti tleft"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:308px;max-width:308px"><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:152px;max-width:152px"><div class="thumbimage"><a href="/wiki/File:Comtemporary_Ghanaian_men_fashion.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/b/b4/Comtemporary_Ghanaian_men_fashion.jpg/150px-Comtemporary_Ghanaian_men_fashion.jpg" decoding="async" width="150" height="150" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/b/b4/Comtemporary_Ghanaian_men_fashion.jpg/225px-Comtemporary_Ghanaian_men_fashion.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/b/b4/Comtemporary_Ghanaian_men_fashion.jpg/300px-Comtemporary_Ghanaian_men_fashion.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2000" data-file-height="2000" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption">Contemporary Ghanaian men's fashion with Kente and other traditional styles</div></div><div class="tsingle" style="width:152px;max-width:152px"><div class="thumbimage"><a href="/wiki/File:Comtemporary_Ghanaian_womenswear.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/1/17/Comtemporary_Ghanaian_womenswear.jpg/150px-Comtemporary_Ghanaian_womenswear.jpg" decoding="async" width="150" height="150" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/1/17/Comtemporary_Ghanaian_womenswear.jpg/225px-Comtemporary_Ghanaian_womenswear.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/1/17/Comtemporary_Ghanaian_womenswear.jpg/300px-Comtemporary_Ghanaian_womenswear.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2000" data-file-height="2000" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption">Contemporary Ghanaian women's fashion with African print/Ankara and other fabrics</div></div></div></div></div>
<p>Contemporary Ghanaian fashion includes traditional and modern styles and fabrics and has made its way into the African and global fashion scene. The cloth known as <a href="/wiki/African_wax_prints" title="African wax prints">African print fabric</a> was created out of Dutch wax textiles. It is believed that in the late 19th century, Dutch ships on their way to Asia stocked with machine-made textiles that mimicked Indonesian <a href="/wiki/Batik" title="Batik">batik</a> stopped at many West African ports on the way. The fabrics did not do well in Asia. However, in West Africa—mainly Ghana where there was an already established market for cloths and textiles—the client base grew and it was changed to include local and traditional designs, colours and patterns to cater to the taste of the new consumers.<sup id="cite_ref-244" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-244">&#91;241&#93;</a></sup> Today outside of Africa it is called "Ankara," and it has a client base well beyond Ghana and Africa as a whole. It is popular among Caribbean peoples and African Americans; celebrities such as <a href="/wiki/Solange_Knowles" title="Solange Knowles">Solange Knowles</a> and her sister <a href="/wiki/Beyonc%C3%A9" title="Beyoncé">Beyoncé</a> have been seen wearing African print attire.<sup id="cite_ref-245" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-245">&#91;242&#93;</a></sup> Many designers from countries in North America and Europe are now using African prints, and they have gained a global interest.<sup id="cite_ref-246" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-246">&#91;243&#93;</a></sup> British luxury fashion house <a href="/wiki/Burberry" title="Burberry">Burberry</a> created a collection around Ghanaian styles.<sup id="cite_ref-247" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-247">&#91;244&#93;</a></sup> American musician <a href="/wiki/Gwen_Stefani" title="Gwen Stefani">Gwen Stefani</a> has repeatedly incorporated African prints into her clothing line and can often be seen wearing it.<sup id="cite_ref-248" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-248">&#91;245&#93;</a></sup> Internationally acclaimed Ghanaian-British designer <a href="/wiki/Ozwald_Boateng" title="Ozwald Boateng">Ozwald Boateng</a> introduced African print suits in his 2012 collection.<sup id="cite_ref-249" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-249">&#91;246&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Music_and_dance">Music and dance</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ghana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=34" title="Edit section: Music and dance">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Azonto" title="Azonto">Azonto</a> and <a href="/wiki/Kpanlogo" title="Kpanlogo">Kpanlogo</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><video id="mwe_player_2" poster="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/85/Traditional_Adowa_dance_form_and_music_performance.ogv/220px--Traditional_Adowa_dance_form_and_music_performance.ogv.jpg" controls="" preload="none" class="thumbimage" width="220" height="293" data-durationhint="16" data-mwtitle="Traditional_Adowa_dance_form_and_music_performance.ogv" data-mwprovider="wikimediacommons"><source src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/8/85/Traditional_Adowa_dance_form_and_music_performance.ogv/Traditional_Adowa_dance_form_and_music_performance.ogv.480p.webm" type="video/webm; codecs=&quot;vp8, vorbis&quot;" data-title="WebM (360P)" data-shorttitle="WebM 360P" data-transcodekey="360p.webm" data-width="270" data-height="360" data-bandwidth="588144" data-framerate="29.97002997003" /><source src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/8/85/Traditional_Adowa_dance_form_and_music_performance.ogv/Traditional_Adowa_dance_form_and_music_performance.ogv.360p.vp9.webm" type="video/webm; codecs=&quot;vp9, opus&quot;" data-title="VP9 (360P)" data-shorttitle="VP9 360P" data-transcodekey="360p.vp9.webm" data-width="270" data-height="360" data-bandwidth="710624" data-framerate="29.97002997003" /></video>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Traditional_Adowa_dance_form_and_music_performance.ogv" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Traditional <a href="/wiki/Adowa_dance" title="Adowa dance">Adowa dance</a> form and music performance.</div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Music_of_Ghana" title="Music of Ghana">music of Ghana</a> is diverse and varies between different ethnic groups and regions. Ghanaian music incorporates several distinct types of musical instruments such as the talking drum ensembles, <a href="/wiki/Akan_Drum" title="Akan Drum">Akan Drum</a>, <a href="/wiki/Goje" title="Goje">goje</a> fiddle and koloko lute, court music, including the Akan <a href="/wiki/Seperewa" title="Seperewa">Seperewa</a>, the Akan atumpan, the Ga kpanlogo styles, and log <a href="/wiki/Xylophone" title="Xylophone">xylophones</a> used in asonko music.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana:_From_Highlife_to_Hiplife_250-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana:_From_Highlife_to_Hiplife-250">&#91;247&#93;</a></sup> The most well-known genres to have come from Ghana are African jazz, which was created by <a href="/wiki/Guy_Warren" title="Guy Warren">Kofi Ghanaba</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-251" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-251">&#91;248&#93;</a></sup> and its earliest form of secular music, called <a href="/wiki/Highlife" title="Highlife">highlife</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana:_From_Highlife_to_Hiplife_250-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana:_From_Highlife_to_Hiplife-250">&#91;247&#93;</a></sup> Highlife originated in the late 19th century and early 20th century and spread throughout West Africa.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana:_From_Highlife_to_Hiplife_250-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana:_From_Highlife_to_Hiplife-250">&#91;247&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p>In the 1990s, a new genre of music was created by the youth incorporating the influences of highlife, Afro-reggae, <a href="/wiki/Dancehall" title="Dancehall">dancehall</a> and <a href="/wiki/Hip_hop_music" title="Hip hop music">hip hop</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana:_From_Highlife_to_Hiplife_250-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana:_From_Highlife_to_Hiplife-250">&#91;247&#93;</a></sup> This hybrid was called <a href="/wiki/Hiplife" title="Hiplife">hiplife</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana:_From_Highlife_to_Hiplife_250-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana:_From_Highlife_to_Hiplife-250">&#91;247&#93;</a></sup> Ghanaian artists such as "Afro Roots" singer, activist and songwriter <a href="/wiki/Rocky_Dawuni" title="Rocky Dawuni">Rocky Dawuni</a>, R&amp;B and soul singer <a href="/wiki/Rhian_Benson" title="Rhian Benson">Rhian Benson</a> and <a href="/wiki/Sarkodie_(rapper)" title="Sarkodie (rapper)">Sarkodie</a> have had international success.<sup id="cite_ref-Rhian_Benson&#39;s_global_soul_sound_252-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Rhian_Benson&#39;s_global_soul_sound-252">&#91;249&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Sarkodie_253-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sarkodie-253">&#91;250&#93;</a></sup> In 2015, Rocky Dawuni became the first Ghanaian musician to be nominated for a Grammy award in the <a href="/wiki/Grammy_Award_for_Best_Reggae_Album" title="Grammy Award for Best Reggae Album">Grammy Award for Best Reggae Album</a> category for his sixth studio album, titled <i>Branches of The Same Tree.</i><sup id="cite_ref-254" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-254">&#91;251&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Ghanaian dance is as diverse as its music, and there are traditional dances and different dances for different occasions.<sup id="cite_ref-Dance,_Ghana_255-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Dance,_Ghana-255">&#91;252&#93;</a></sup> The most known dances are those for celebrations. These dances include the <a href="/wiki/Adowa_dance" title="Adowa dance">Adowa</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kpanlogo" title="Kpanlogo">Kpanlogo</a>, <a href="/wiki/Azonto" title="Azonto">Azonto</a>, <a href="/w/index.php?title=Klama&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Klama (page does not exist)">Klama</a>, <a href="/wiki/Agbadza" title="Agbadza">Agbadza</a>, <a href="/wiki/Borborbor" title="Borborbor">Borborbor</a> and <a href="/w/index.php?title=Bamaya&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Bamaya (page does not exist)">Bamaya</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Dance,_Ghana_255-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Dance,_Ghana-255">&#91;252&#93;</a></sup> The Nana Otafrija Pallbearing Services, also known as the <a href="/wiki/Dancing_Pallbearers" title="Dancing Pallbearers">Dancing Pallbearers</a>, come from the coastal town of <a href="/wiki/Prampram" title="Prampram">Prampram</a>. The group was featured in a BBC feature story in 2017, and footage from the story became part of an Internet meme in the wake of the <a href="/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic" title="COVID-19 pandemic">COVID-19 world pandemic</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-256" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-256">&#91;253&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Film">Film</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ghana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=35" title="Edit section: Film">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti"/><div class="thumb tmulti tleft"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:319px;max-width:319px"><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:101px;max-width:101px"><div class="thumbimage"><a href="/wiki/File:Van_Vicker_(Cropped).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d7/Van_Vicker_%28Cropped%29.jpg/99px-Van_Vicker_%28Cropped%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="99" height="144" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d7/Van_Vicker_%28Cropped%29.jpg/149px-Van_Vicker_%28Cropped%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/d/d7/Van_Vicker_%28Cropped%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="179" data-file-height="261" /></a></div></div><div class="tsingle" style="width:110px;max-width:110px"><div class="thumbimage"><a href="/wiki/File:Boris_Kodjoe_2010_c.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/Boris_Kodjoe_2010_c.jpg/108px-Boris_Kodjoe_2010_c.jpg" decoding="async" width="108" height="144" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/Boris_Kodjoe_2010_c.jpg/162px-Boris_Kodjoe_2010_c.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/Boris_Kodjoe_2010_c.jpg/216px-Boris_Kodjoe_2010_c.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1200" data-file-height="1600" /></a></div></div><div class="tsingle" style="width:102px;max-width:102px"><div class="thumbimage"><a href="/wiki/File:Idris_Elba_2014.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/Idris_Elba_2014.jpg/100px-Idris_Elba_2014.jpg" decoding="async" width="100" height="144" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/Idris_Elba_2014.jpg/150px-Idris_Elba_2014.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/Idris_Elba_2014.jpg/200px-Idris_Elba_2014.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1350" data-file-height="1944" /></a></div></div></div><div class="trow" style="display:flow-root"><div class="thumbcaption" style="text-align:center">Popular <a href="/wiki/List_of_Ghanaian_actors" title="List of Ghanaian actors">actor of Ghanaian ancestry</a>, <a href="/wiki/Van_Vicker" title="Van Vicker">Van Vicker</a>, and international actors <a href="/wiki/Boris_Kodjoe" title="Boris Kodjoe">Boris Kodjoe</a> and <a href="/wiki/Idris_Elba" title="Idris Elba">Idris Elba</a></div></div></div></div>
<p>Ghana has a budding and thriving film industry. <a href="/wiki/Cinema_of_Ghana" title="Cinema of Ghana">Ghana's film industry</a> dates as far back as 1948 when the Gold Coast Film Unit was set up in the Information Services Department.<sup id="cite_ref-257" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-257">&#91;254&#93;</a></sup> Some internationally recognised films have come from Ghana. In 1970, <i>I Told You So</i> was one of the first Ghanaian films to receive international acknowledgement and received great reviews from <i><a href="/wiki/The_New_York_Times" title="The New York Times">The New York Times</a></i>.<sup id="cite_ref-258" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-258">&#91;255&#93;</a></sup> It was followed by the 1973 Ghanaian and Italian production <i>The African Deal</i> also known as "<i>Contratto carnale</i>" featuring Bahamian American actor <a href="/wiki/Calvin_Lockhart" title="Calvin Lockhart">Calvin Lockhart</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-259" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-259">&#91;256&#93;</a></sup> 1983's <i>Kukurantumi: the Road to Accra</i>, a Ghanaian and German production directed by King Ampaw, was written about by famous American film critic <a href="/wiki/Vincent_Canby" title="Vincent Canby">Vincent Canby</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-260" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-260">&#91;257&#93;</a></sup> In 1987, <i><a href="/wiki/Cobra_Verde" title="Cobra Verde">Cobra Verde</a></i>, another Ghanaian and German production directed by <a href="/wiki/Werner_Herzog" title="Werner Herzog">Werner Herzog</a>, received international acclamation and in 1988, <i>Heritage Africa</i> won more than 12 film awards.
</p><p>In recent times, there have been collaborations between Ghanaian and Nigerian crew and cast and a number of productions turned out. Many Ghanaian films are co-produced with <a href="/wiki/Cinema_of_Nigeria" title="Cinema of Nigeria">Nollywood</a>, the Nigerian film industry, and some are distributed by Nigerian marketers. Also, Nigerian filmmakers often feature Ghanaian actors and actresses in their movies, and Ghanaian filmmakers feature Nigerian actors and actresses in theirs. <a href="/wiki/Nadia_Buari" title="Nadia Buari">Nadia Buari</a>, <a href="/wiki/Yvonne_Nelson" title="Yvonne Nelson">Yvonne Nelson</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lydia_Forson" title="Lydia Forson">Lydia Forson</a> and <a href="/wiki/Jackie_Appiah" title="Jackie Appiah">Jackie Appiah</a>, all popular Ghanaian actresses, and <a href="/wiki/Van_Vicker" title="Van Vicker">Van Vicker</a> and <a href="/wiki/Majid_Michel" title="Majid Michel">Majid Michel</a> are popular Ghanaian actors, have starred in many Nigerian movies. As a result of these collaborations, Western viewers often confuse Ghanaian movies with Nollywood and count their sales as one; however, they are two independent industries that sometimes share <i>Nollywood</i>. In 2009, UNESCO described Nollywood as the second-biggest film industry in the world after <a href="/wiki/Hindi_cinema" title="Hindi cinema">Bollywood</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-261" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-261">&#91;258&#93;</a></sup> Though The film industry had a downtrend for almost a decade mainly because of low input in production this scenario has drastically changed. New and emerging young film makers are adding spice to the already rich Ghana movie scene. Bliz Bazawule,<sup id="cite_ref-262" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-262">&#91;259&#93;</a></sup> Peter Sedufia,<sup id="cite_ref-263" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-263">&#91;260&#93;</a></sup> Joseph Clef<sup id="cite_ref-264" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-264">&#91;261&#93;</a></sup> and many others have shown the world the new age of filming in Ghana.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Media">Media</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ghana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=36" title="Edit section: Media">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Ghana_Trustworthiness_of_Media.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5e/Ghana_Trustworthiness_of_Media.jpg/220px-Ghana_Trustworthiness_of_Media.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="153" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5e/Ghana_Trustworthiness_of_Media.jpg/330px-Ghana_Trustworthiness_of_Media.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5e/Ghana_Trustworthiness_of_Media.jpg/440px-Ghana_Trustworthiness_of_Media.jpg 2x" data-file-width="445" data-file-height="310" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Ghana_Trustworthiness_of_Media.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Ghana mass media, news and information provided by television.</div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Media_of_Ghana" class="mw-redirect" title="Media of Ghana">media of Ghana</a> are amongst the most free in Africa. Chapter 12 of the 1992 Constitution of Ghana guarantees <a href="/wiki/Freedom_of_the_press" title="Freedom of the press">freedom of the press</a> and independence of the media, while Chapter 2 prohibits censorship.<sup id="cite_ref-gov_265-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gov-265">&#91;262&#93;</a></sup> Post-independence, the government and media often had a tense relationship, with private outlets closed during the military governments and strict media laws that prevented criticism of government.<sup id="cite_ref-Anokwa_266-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Anokwa-266">&#91;263&#93;</a></sup> Press freedoms were restored in 1992, and after the election in 2000 of Kufuor, the tensions between the private media and government decreased. Kufuor supported press freedom and repealed a <a href="/wiki/Defamation" title="Defamation">libel</a> law, but maintained that the media had to act responsibly.<sup id="cite_ref-pr_267-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pr-267">&#91;264&#93;</a></sup> The Ghanaian media have been described as "one of the most unfettered" in Africa, operating with little restriction. The private press often carries criticism of government policy.<sup id="cite_ref-bbc_268-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc-268">&#91;265&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sports">Sports</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ghana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=37" title="Edit section: Sports">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Sports_in_Ghana" title="Sports in Ghana">Sports in Ghana</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Ghana_at_the_2010_Winter_Olympics" title="Ghana at the 2010 Winter Olympics">Ghana at the Winter Olympics</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ghana_at_the_Olympics" title="Ghana at the Olympics">Ghana at the Olympics</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:20150331_Mali_vs_Ghana_042.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/20150331_Mali_vs_Ghana_042.jpg/220px-20150331_Mali_vs_Ghana_042.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/20150331_Mali_vs_Ghana_042.jpg/330px-20150331_Mali_vs_Ghana_042.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/20150331_Mali_vs_Ghana_042.jpg/440px-20150331_Mali_vs_Ghana_042.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3599" data-file-height="2400" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:20150331_Mali_vs_Ghana_042.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Black Stars, the <a href="/wiki/Ghana_national_football_team" title="Ghana national football team">Ghana national football team</a></div></div></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Association_football" title="Association football">Association football</a> is the top spectator sport in Ghana, and the <a href="/wiki/Ghana_national_football_team" title="Ghana national football team">national men's football team</a> is known as the Black Stars, with the <a href="/wiki/Ghana_national_under-20_football_team" title="Ghana national under-20 football team">under-20 team</a> known as the Black Satellites.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_thrilled_by_historic_title_269-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_thrilled_by_historic_title-269">&#91;266&#93;</a></sup> Ghana has won the <a href="/wiki/Africa_Cup_of_Nations" title="Africa Cup of Nations">Africa Cup of Nations</a> four times, the <a href="/wiki/FIFA_U-20_World_Cup" title="FIFA U-20 World Cup">FIFA U-20 World Cup</a> once, and has participated in three consecutive <a href="/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup" title="FIFA World Cup">FIFA World Cups</a> in 2006, 2010, and 2014.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_thrilled_by_historic_title_269-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_thrilled_by_historic_title-269">&#91;266&#93;</a></sup> In the <a href="/wiki/2010_FIFA_World_Cup" title="2010 FIFA World Cup">2010 FIFA World Cup</a>, Ghana became the third African country to reach the quarter-final stage after Cameroon in 1990 and Senegal in 2002.<sup id="cite_ref-270" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-270">&#91;267&#93;</a></sup> Ghana is the first and only country on the Africa continent to be crowned FIFA U-20 World Cup Champions,<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_thrilled_by_historic_title_269-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_thrilled_by_historic_title-269">&#91;266&#93;</a></sup> and two-time runners up in <a href="/wiki/1993_FIFA_World_Youth_Championship" title="1993 FIFA World Youth Championship">1993</a> and <a href="/wiki/2001_FIFA_World_Youth_Championship" title="2001 FIFA World Youth Championship">2001</a>. The Ghana national U-17 football team known as the Black Starlets are two-time <a href="/wiki/FIFA_U-17_World_Cup" title="FIFA U-17 World Cup">FIFA U-17 World Cup</a> champions in <a href="/wiki/1991_FIFA_U-17_World_Championship" title="1991 FIFA U-17 World Championship">1991</a> and <a href="/wiki/1995_FIFA_U-17_World_Championship" title="1995 FIFA U-17 World Championship">1995</a>, two-time runners up in <a href="/wiki/1993_FIFA_U-17_World_Championship" title="1993 FIFA U-17 World Championship">1993</a> and <a href="/wiki/1997_FIFA_World_Youth_Championship" title="1997 FIFA World Youth Championship">1997</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-271" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-271">&#91;268&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Black_Stars_Goal_Celebration_(Ghana_national_football_team).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3f/Black_Stars_Goal_Celebration_%28Ghana_national_football_team%29.jpg/220px-Black_Stars_Goal_Celebration_%28Ghana_national_football_team%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3f/Black_Stars_Goal_Celebration_%28Ghana_national_football_team%29.jpg/330px-Black_Stars_Goal_Celebration_%28Ghana_national_football_team%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3f/Black_Stars_Goal_Celebration_%28Ghana_national_football_team%29.jpg/440px-Black_Stars_Goal_Celebration_%28Ghana_national_football_team%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="533" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Black_Stars_Goal_Celebration_(Ghana_national_football_team).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A Black Stars <a href="/wiki/Goal_celebration" title="Goal celebration">goal celebration</a></div></div></div>
<p>Ghanaian <a href="/wiki/Football_team" title="Football team">football teams</a> <a href="/wiki/Asante_Kotoko_S.C." title="Asante Kotoko S.C.">Asante Kotoko SC</a> and <a href="/wiki/Accra_Hearts_of_Oak_S.C." title="Accra Hearts of Oak S.C.">Accra Hearts of Oak SC</a> are the 5th and 9th best football teams on the Africa continent and have won a total of five Africa continental association football and <a href="/wiki/Confederation_of_African_Football" title="Confederation of African Football">Confederation of African Football</a> trophies; Asante Kotoko SC has been crowned two-time <a href="/wiki/CAF_Champions_League" title="CAF Champions League">CAF Champions League</a> winners in <a href="/wiki/1970_African_Cup_of_Champions_Clubs" title="1970 African Cup of Champions Clubs">1970</a>, <a href="/wiki/1983_African_Cup_of_Champions_Clubs" title="1983 African Cup of Champions Clubs">1983</a> and five-time CAF Champions League runners up. Accra Hearts of Oak SC has been crowned <a href="/wiki/2000_CAF_Champions_League" title="2000 CAF Champions League">2000 CAF Champions League</a> winner and two-time CAF Champions League runners up, 2001 <a href="/wiki/CAF_Super_Cup" title="CAF Super Cup">CAF Super Cup</a> champions and <a href="/wiki/2004_CAF_Confederation_Cup" title="2004 CAF Confederation Cup">2004 CAF Confederation Cup</a> champions.<sup id="cite_ref-Africa&#39;s_club_of_the_Century_272-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Africa&#39;s_club_of_the_Century-272">&#91;269&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/International_Federation_of_Football_History_%26_Statistics" title="International Federation of Football History &amp; Statistics">International Federation of Football History and Statistics</a> crowned Asante Kotoko SC as the <a href="/wiki/International_Federation_of_Football_History_%26_Statistics#Continental_Clubs_of_the_20th_century" title="International Federation of Football History &amp; Statistics">African club of the 20th century</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Africa&#39;s_club_of_the_Century_272-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Africa&#39;s_club_of_the_Century-272">&#91;269&#93;</a></sup> There are several club football teams that play in the <a href="/wiki/Ghana_Premier_League" title="Ghana Premier League">Ghana Premier League</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ghana_Football_Leagues" title="Ghana Football Leagues">Division One League</a>, both administered by the <a href="/wiki/Ghana_Football_Association" title="Ghana Football Association">Ghana Football Association</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-273" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-273">&#91;270&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:2010_Opening_Ceremony_-_Ghana_entering.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/2010_Opening_Ceremony_-_Ghana_entering.jpg/220px-2010_Opening_Ceremony_-_Ghana_entering.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="220" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/2010_Opening_Ceremony_-_Ghana_entering.jpg/330px-2010_Opening_Ceremony_-_Ghana_entering.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/2010_Opening_Ceremony_-_Ghana_entering.jpg/440px-2010_Opening_Ceremony_-_Ghana_entering.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1207" data-file-height="1206" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:2010_Opening_Ceremony_-_Ghana_entering.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Ghanaian <a href="/wiki/Winter_sport" class="mw-redirect" title="Winter sport">winter sports</a> Olympic team at the <a href="/wiki/Opening_ceremony" title="Opening ceremony">opening ceremony</a> of the 2010 <a href="/wiki/Winter_Olympics" class="mw-redirect" title="Winter Olympics">Winter Olympics</a></div></div></div>
<p>Ghana competed in the Winter Olympics in 2010 for the first time. Ghana qualified for the 2010 Winter Olympics, scoring 137.5 <a href="/wiki/International_Ski_Federation" title="International Ski Federation">International Ski Federation</a> points, within the qualifying range of 120–140 points.<sup id="cite_ref-0-21_274-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-0-21-274">&#91;271&#93;</a></sup> Skier <a href="/wiki/Kwame_Nkrumah-Acheampong" title="Kwame Nkrumah-Acheampong">Kwame Nkrumah-Acheampong</a> became the first Ghanaian to take part in the Winter Olympics, taking part in the <a href="/wiki/Slalom_skiing" title="Slalom skiing">slalom skiing</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Vancouver_2010_275-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Vancouver_2010-275">&#91;272&#93;</a></sup> Ghana finished 47th out of 102 participating nations, of whom 54 finished in the <a href="/wiki/Alpine_skiing_at_the_2010_Winter_Olympics_%E2%80%93_Men%27s_slalom" title="Alpine skiing at the 2010 Winter Olympics – Men&#39;s slalom">Alpine skiing slalom</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-276" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-276">&#91;273&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Results_277-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Results-277">&#91;274&#93;</a></sup> Ghana's last medal at the <a href="/wiki/Summer_Olympic_Games" title="Summer Olympic Games">Summer Olympics</a> dates back to 1992.<sup id="cite_ref-278" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-278">&#91;275&#93;</a></sup> Ghanaian athletes have won a total of four medals in thirteen appearances at the Summer Olympics, three in boxing, and a bronze medal in association football, and thus became the first country on the Africa continent to win a medal at association football.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_Olympic_279-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_Olympic-279">&#91;276&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Ghana competes in the <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_Games" title="Commonwealth Games">Commonwealth Games</a>, sending athletes in every edition since <a href="/wiki/1954_British_Empire_and_Commonwealth_Games" title="1954 British Empire and Commonwealth Games">1954</a> (except for the <a href="/wiki/1986_Commonwealth_Games" title="1986 Commonwealth Games">1986 games</a>). Ghana has won 57 medals at the Commonwealth Games, including 15 gold, with all but one of their medals coming in athletics and boxing. The country has also produced a number of world class boxers, including <a href="/wiki/Azumah_Nelson" title="Azumah Nelson">Azumah Nelson</a> a three-time world champion and considered as Africa's greatest boxer,<sup id="cite_ref-280" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-280">&#91;277&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Top_5_Ghanaian_Boxers_281-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Top_5_Ghanaian_Boxers-281">&#91;278&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Nana_Konadu" title="Nana Konadu">Nana Yaw Konadu</a> also a three-time world champion,<sup id="cite_ref-Top_5_Ghanaian_Boxers_281-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Top_5_Ghanaian_Boxers-281">&#91;278&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Ike_Quartey" title="Ike Quartey">Ike Quartey</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-Top_5_Ghanaian_Boxers_281-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Top_5_Ghanaian_Boxers-281">&#91;278&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Joshua_Clottey" title="Joshua Clottey">Joshua Clottey</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Top_5_Ghanaian_Boxers_281-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Top_5_Ghanaian_Boxers-281">&#91;278&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>
Ghana's women's football team won a bronze at the <a href="/wiki/Women%27s_Africa_Cup_of_Nations" title="Women&#39;s Africa Cup of Nations">Africa Women Cup of Nations</a> 2016 edition in Yaoundé, Cameroon. The team beat South Africa 1–0.<sup id="cite_ref-282" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-282">&#91;279&#93;</a></sup> Ghana featured a men's national team in <a href="/wiki/Beach_volleyball" title="Beach volleyball">beach volleyball</a> that competed at the <a href="/wiki/2018%E2%80%932020_CAVB_Beach_Volleyball_Continental_Cup" title="2018–2020 CAVB Beach Volleyball Continental Cup">2018–2020 CAVB Beach Volleyball Continental Cup</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-283" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-283">&#91;280&#93;</a></sup> Ghana is to host the <a href="/wiki/2023_African_Games" title="2023 African Games">2023 African Games</a> in Accra.</p><div style="clear:both;"></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cultural_heritage_and_architecture">Cultural heritage and architecture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ghana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=38" title="Edit section: Cultural heritage and architecture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Ghana%27s_material_cultural_heritage" title="Ghana&#39;s material cultural heritage">Ghana's material cultural heritage</a> and <a href="/wiki/List_of_museums_in_Ghana" title="List of museums in Ghana">Ghanaian museums</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:A_drone_footage_of_Accra_central,_Ghana.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b6/A_drone_footage_of_Accra_central%2C_Ghana.jpg/220px-A_drone_footage_of_Accra_central%2C_Ghana.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="133" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b6/A_drone_footage_of_Accra_central%2C_Ghana.jpg/330px-A_drone_footage_of_Accra_central%2C_Ghana.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b6/A_drone_footage_of_Accra_central%2C_Ghana.jpg/440px-A_drone_footage_of_Accra_central%2C_Ghana.jpg 2x" data-file-width="960" data-file-height="579" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:A_drone_footage_of_Accra_central,_Ghana.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Modern <a href="/wiki/High-rise_building" class="mw-redirect" title="High-rise building">high-rise buildings</a> in <a href="/wiki/Accra" title="Accra">Accra</a>, the capital</div></div></div>
<p>There are two types of Ghanaian traditional construction: the series of adjacent buildings in an enclosure around a common, and the traditional round huts with grass roof.<sup id="cite_ref-ARC_284-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ARC-284">&#91;281&#93;</a></sup> The round huts with grass roof architecture are situated in the northern regions, while the series of adjacent buildings are in the southern regions. Ghanaian <a href="/wiki/Postmodern_architecture" title="Postmodern architecture">postmodern architecture</a> and <a href="/wiki/High-tech_architecture" title="High-tech architecture">high-tech architecture</a> buildings are predominant in the southern regions, while heritage sites are most evident in the more than thirty forts and castles in the country, such as <a href="/wiki/Fort_William,_Ghana" title="Fort William, Ghana">Fort William</a> and <a href="/wiki/Fort_Amsterdam,_Ghana" title="Fort Amsterdam, Ghana">Fort Amsterdam</a>. Ghana has museums that are situated inside castles, and two are situated inside a fort.<sup id="cite_ref-Museums_and_Monuments_Board_285-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Museums_and_Monuments_Board-285">&#91;282&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Armed_Forces_Museum_(Ghana)" title="Armed Forces Museum (Ghana)">Military Museum</a> and the <a href="/wiki/National_Museum_of_Ghana" title="National Museum of Ghana">National Museum</a> organise temporary exhibitions.<sup id="cite_ref-Museums_and_Monuments_Board_285-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Museums_and_Monuments_Board-285">&#91;282&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Ghana has museums that show an in-depth look at specific regions. There are a number of museums that provide insight into the traditions and history of the geographical areas.<sup id="cite_ref-Museums_and_Monuments_Board_285-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Museums_and_Monuments_Board-285">&#91;282&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Cape_Coast_Castle" title="Cape Coast Castle">Cape Coast Castle</a> Museum and St. Georges Castle (<a href="/wiki/Elmina_Castle" title="Elmina Castle">Elmina Castle</a>) Museum offer guided tours. The <a href="/wiki/Kwame_Nkrumah_University_of_Science_and_Technology" title="Kwame Nkrumah University of Science and Technology">Museum of Science and Technology</a> provides its visitors with a look into the domain of Ghanaian scientific development, through exhibits of objects of scientific and technological interest.<sup id="cite_ref-Museums_and_Monuments_Board_285-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Museums_and_Monuments_Board-285">&#91;282&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="National_symbols">National symbols</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Ghana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=39" title="Edit section: National symbols">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Tawny_Eagle_(Aquila_rapax)_1.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/44/Tawny_Eagle_%28Aquila_rapax%29_1.jpg/220px-Tawny_Eagle_%28Aquila_rapax%29_1.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/44/Tawny_Eagle_%28Aquila_rapax%29_1.jpg/330px-Tawny_Eagle_%28Aquila_rapax%29_1.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/44/Tawny_Eagle_%28Aquila_rapax%29_1.jpg/440px-Tawny_Eagle_%28Aquila_rapax%29_1.jpg 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="531" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Tawny_Eagle_(Aquila_rapax)_1.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><div class="center" style="width:auto; margin-left:auto; margin-right:auto;">The <a href="/wiki/Tawny_eagle" title="Tawny eagle">tawny eagle</a> appears on the <a href="/wiki/Coat_of_arms_of_Ghana" title="Coat of arms of Ghana">coat of arms of Ghana</a>.</div></div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Coat_of_arms_of_Ghana" title="Coat of arms of Ghana">coat of arms</a> depicts two animals: the <a href="/wiki/Tawny_eagle" title="Tawny eagle">tawny eagle</a> and the lion; a ceremonial sword; a heraldic castle on a heraldic sea; a <a href="/wiki/Theobroma_cacao" title="Theobroma cacao">cocoa tree</a> and a mine shaft representing the industrial mineral wealth of Ghana; and a five-pointed black star rimmed with gold, representing the mineral gold wealth of Ghana and the lodestar of the Ghanaian people.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_National_Emblems_286-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_National_Emblems-286">&#91;283&#93;</a></sup> It also has the legend <i>Freedom and Justice</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_National_Emblems_286-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_National_Emblems-286">&#91;283&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>
The <a href="/wiki/Flag_of_Ghana" title="Flag of Ghana">flag of Ghana</a> consists of three horizontal bands (strips) of red (top), gold (middle) and green (bottom); the three bands are the same height and width; the middle band bears a five-pointed black <a href="/wiki/Star" title="Star">star</a> in the centre of the gold band, the colour red band stands for the blood spilled to achieve the nation's independence: gold stands for Ghana's industrial mineral wealth, and the colour green symbolises the rich tropical rainforests and natural resources of Ghana.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_flag_and_description_45-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_flag_and_description-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_National_Emblems_286-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_National_Emblems-286">&#91;283&#93;</a></sup></p><div style="clear:both;"></div>
<div class="thumb tnone" style="margin-left:auto;margin-right:auto;overflow:hidden;width:auto;max-width:608px"><div class="thumbinner"><div class="noresize" style="overflow:auto"><a href="/wiki/File:Panorama_view_of_Kakum_Nationalpark.jpg" class="image" title="View of Kakum National Park"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fc/Panorama_view_of_Kakum_Nationalpark.jpg/600px-Panorama_view_of_Kakum_Nationalpark.jpg" decoding="async" width="600" height="179" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fc/Panorama_view_of_Kakum_Nationalpark.jpg/900px-Panorama_view_of_Kakum_Nationalpark.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fc/Panorama_view_of_Kakum_Nationalpark.jpg/1200px-Panorama_view_of_Kakum_Nationalpark.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3621" data-file-height="1080" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption" style="text-align:center"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Panorama_view_of_Kakum_Nationalpark.jpg" title="File:Panorama view of Kakum Nationalpark.jpg"> </a></div>View of <a href="/wiki/Kakum_National_Park" title="Kakum National Park">Kakum National Park</a></div></div></div>
<div style="clear:both;"></div>

<p><b><a href="/wiki/Geographic_coordinate_system" title="Geographic coordinate system">Lat. <small>and</small> Long.</a> <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1073938472"/><span class="plainlinks nourlexpansion"><a class="external text" href="//geohack.toolforge.org/geohack.php?pagename=Ghana&amp;params=5_33_N_0_12_W_"><span class="geo-default"><span class="geo-dms" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location"><span class="latitude">5°33′N</span> <span class="longitude">0°12′W</span></span></span><span class="geo-multi-punct">&#xfeff; / &#xfeff;</span><span class="geo-nondefault"><span class="geo-dec" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location">5.550°N 0.200°W</span><span style="display:none">&#xfeff; / <span class="geo">5.550; -0.200</span></span></span></a></span> <span style="color:darkblue;">(Accra)</span></b><br /><b><a href="/wiki/Geographic_coordinate_system" title="Geographic coordinate system">Lat. <small>and</small> Long.</a> <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1073938472"/><span class="plainlinks nourlexpansion"><a class="external text" href="//geohack.toolforge.org/geohack.php?pagename=Ghana&amp;params=6_40_N_1_37_W_"><span class="geo-default"><span class="geo-dms" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location"><span class="latitude">6°40′N</span> <span class="longitude">1°37′W</span></span></span><span class="geo-multi-punct">&#xfeff; / &#xfeff;</span><span class="geo-nondefault"><span class="geo-dec" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location">6.667°N 1.617°W</span><span style="display:none">&#xfeff; / <span class="geo">6.667; -1.617</span></span></span></a></span> <span style="color:darkblue;">(Kumasi)</span></b>
